# Source ledger

Checked 27 September 2026. Links are to the actual item where possible. A transcription, obituary, research compilation, and family chart carry different evidentiary weight.

| ID | Source and what it supports | Type / limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F1 | [Supplied four-page chart](../sources/family/Kramer%20family%20tree.doc): proposed names, dates, parent links. See [transcription](chart-transcription.md). | Family compilation; provenance of original entries absent, internal errors. |
| F2 | [Shared ChatGPT conversation](https://chatgpt.com/share/6ab81041-507c-83e9-a26a-2b158812080a): oral-history context and research leads. | Lead only; its conclusions are rechecked against linked sources here. A second ChatGPT link mentioned inside it did not expose content. |
| U1 | [Boyd's Wilkes-Barre directory extracts](https://pagenweb.org/~luzerne/towns/donnacd.htm): 1878–79 Matthew mason; 1888 Matthew laborer, 22 Susquehanna; 1889 Kate silk-mill beamer, Lenta and Martin at 22 Susquehanna; 1898 Matthias ropemaker and Julia (book sewer), Marcus G./Martin (ropemakers), Mary (dressmaker) at 22 Susquehanna; 1900 Matthias, Julia (binder), Martin and Mary C. at 17 Susquehanna; 1904 Ferdinand, Martin, Martin Jr. (laborers) and William F. (bottler) at 17 Susquehanna; 1914 Ferdinand laborer, 146 Prospect. [1871 directory](https://pagenweb.org/~luzerne/towns/1871city.htm) has “Krammer, Matthias” mason. | Volunteer transcription of historical directories, strong for listings; not a proof of relationships or same-person identity. Verify against scans. |
| U2 | [Hilda M. Wolosz obituary](https://www.legacy.com/us/obituaries/citizensvoice/name/hilda-wolosz-obituary?id=7822560): parents Ferdinand and Rose Bosch Kramer, brothers Ferdinand and Paul, G.A.R. 1940, insurance work. | Family/funeral-home obituary published by *Citizens' Voice*, 2020. |

The historian says the 1847 auction apparently did not complete then. The municipal account says the 1767/68 seller was a **Kramer** family; no relationship to Justin’s Kramer ancestors is established. The 1284 mention concerns the site, not the family. |
| G4 | [Institute for Palatinate History, Magdalena Fröhlich card 98001](https://migration.pfalzgeschichte.de/person/98001): birth, spouse, seven named children, 1864 emigration with four children, claimed parents Casimir Fröhlich and Katharina Rauschel. Linked [Johann Ludwig Disque card 98000](https://migration.pfalzgeschichte.de/person/98000) gives differing 1796/1853 dates; [Johannes Ludwig card 98258](https://migration.pfalzgeschichte.de/person/98258) documents a son in Wilkes-Barre; [Margaretha card 98304](https://migration.pfalzgeschichte.de/person/98304) supplies migration route details. | Institutional index citing `QAF-Disque, 2008` and *Germans to America* 16:37 for emigration; originals unseen. Seven-child list omits chart Magdalena, while Fröhlich parents and Johann dates conflict with chart. |

| U15 | [1950 U.S. census image, Pennsylvania, Luzerne County, Wilkes-Barre, ED 80-125, sheet 4, lines 25–29](https://1950census.archives.gov/iiif/2/1950census%2F43290879-Pennsylvania%2F43290879-Pennsylvania-135713%2F43290879-Pennsylvania-135713-0005.jpg/full/full/0/default.jpg): Ferdinand (36), Mary (33), sons Ferdinand (recorded 11) and Paul (5), father-in-law Frank Andrews (62). Ferdinand worked as a machine operator at a wire-rope works. Mary's occupation field says machine operator in a dress factory, while her last-week status says keeping house and her sampled answer reports no 1949 wages. The sample also says she completed seventh grade and had borne four children. [NARA district manifest](https://1950census.archives.gov/api/manifest?ed=80-125&state=PA). | Original federal census image viewed, not just AI name extraction. Strong household match to U3, but no middle names. Son's stated age conflicts by one year with U3's July 1939 birth; two other liveborn children are unnamed. Mary's occupation fields conflict with her reported activity, so current factory employment or a second wage cannot be inferred. |
| U16 | [1950 U.S. census image, Pennsylvania, Luzerne County, Wilkes-Barre, ED 80-71, sheet 13, lines 22–24](https://1950census.archives.gov/iiif/2/1950census%2F43290879-Pennsylvania%2F43290879-Pennsylvania-135659%2F43290879-Pennsylvania-135659-0014.jpg/full/full/0/default.jpg): Ferdinand Kramer (67), widowed, house number 146; Joseph Wolosz (36), son-in-law and garment-factory timekeeper; Hilda (28), daughter. | U28 | [FamilySearch index of Ferdinand Francis “Fred” Kramer's 12 August 2023 *Times Leader* obituary](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:6L83-L29V?lang=en): wife **Patricia Anne Marie Cordaro Kramer**. Patricia's page indexes **Patricia Gould, John Kramer, Karen Horn, and Paul Kramer** as her children; it identifies Ferdinand Louis and Mary Andrews Kramer as **parents-in-law**. | Derivative index of one obituary, viewed in signed-in Chrome. With Justin's statement that Paul is his father, establishes the Cordaro-to-Kramer bridge. It names no parents for Patricia and no passport holder. The listed children are obituary relationships, without birth details. The original image is restricted to a partner/custodian. [Interpretation](../branches/cordaro-passport-lead.md#what-patricias-familysearch-result-actually-contains). |

Patricia's parents are **Joseph Cordaro and Dina Caucci**; Patricia was born **9 June 1940 in Kingston**, and was a secretary. Joseph was born West Wyoming and a laborer; Dina was born Jessup and worked at a dress factory. | Original application and minister's return viewed in signed-in Chrome on 26 September 2026; record is free to view but the archive charges to download. Parent fields are direct applicant statements; precise maiden spelling is reinforced by U30/U31. The record does not name Patricia's sisters or the passport holder. [Analysis](../branches/cordaro-passport-lead.md#three-generation-record-path). |

It names his parents **Anthony Cordaro and Petra Magro** (index *Magra*), and hers **Joseph Caucci and Anna Belardinelli** (index *Belardinelle*). | Original county image downloaded through FamilySearch 26 September 2026; the adjacent left page is another couple. Handwriting causes Magro/Magra and Belardinelli/Belardinelle spelling uncertainty. Joseph was 28 and a laborer; Dena was 20 and born Jessup. Names Dena/Dina vary across records. |
| U31 | [Dina M. Caucci/Cordaro in Social Security NUMIDENT](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:6K95-YJSQ?lang=en): born **25 November 1917**, Jessup; father **Joseph Caucci**; died **9 January 1989**. | Index of federal administrative data, no image. Married and maiden surnames plus birthplace align with U29/U30. FamilySearch tree says 26 November birth, a one-day conflict. Mother is unnamed in this index; Anna comes from U30. |
| U32 | [1920 census, Anthony “Cordra” household](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:M611-V9M?lang=en), West Wyoming, Luzerne County: Anthony and wife Petria born Italy, son Joseph age 10 born Pennsylvania. Arrival columns indexed **1895** for Anthony and **1909** for Petria; daughters Fannie and Mary were Italy-born. [1940 census of Joseph and Dina](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:KQ7W-L64?lang=en) locates them in West Wyoming before Patricia's birth. | Census indexes checked against household composition; the 1920 original sheet should be read for occupations, exact arrival columns and name spellings. Reported years are **arrival leads**, not proven voyages, especially as some children were born in Italy after 1895. |
| U33 | [1899 Italian civil marriage index](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:6NCZ-7RPP?lang=en): Antonio Cordaro, 21, born **Sutera**, and Pietra Magro, 20, born **Milocca**, married at Milocca **1 August 1899**; Antonio's mother indexed **Maria Tona**. | Indexed act; its FamilySearch image is restricted. Earlier notes misidentified that page as another couple; this was a reading error. |

James Blevins** for **£1 8s 8d** on an account; **David Caldwell v. James Blevins** on a debt, with an attached spoon, **£18 10s** face judgment dischargeable by **£9 5s** plus interest and a **£4 10s** prior-payment credit. | Original page images inspected using signed-in FamilySearch, with automated transcript as reading aid. The two defendants may be the same James but neither case gives parentage or ties him to the 1737 grantee. Attachment of one item does not describe his full property or prove poverty. The smaller discharge and credit prevent treating the face judgment as cash owed or wealth lost. |
| B13 | [Original Lunenburg Order Book 2, 2 October 1751, p. 464](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3Q9M-CS4X-941R-Z?view=fullText&lang=en) assigns **William Williams** to maintain the road from Smith River to Leatherwood and **Merry Webb** the next section toward the north fork of Sandy River. The [Virginia Transportation Research Council transcription, pp. 37–38](https://vtrc.virginia.gov/media/vtrc/vtrc-pdf/vtrc-pdf/93-r17.pdf#page=42) independently identifies the date and page. | Original image and FamilySearch transcript inspected in signed-in Chrome; official transcription used as a reading cross-check. This is evidence for the regional road network, **not evidence that James or Daniel Blevin worked on either section**. It does not verify Clement's claim that a surveyor mentioned their separate wagon road. |
| B14 | [Will Graves's transcription of federal Revolutionary pension file S32121](https://revwarapps.org/s32121.pdf), pp. 1–4: James Blevens/Blevins's **12 November 1832** declaration in Lawrence County, Indiana; **1833** testimony of Robert Ellis and John Stone; later six-month service and **$20 annual pension** notation. | A transcription of an original federal file, **not the file images**. The transcriber states that some spellings were normalized and some material omitted. James reports a New England birth, childhood Virginia moves, wartime service and an Indiana move about 1821; the stated ages imply **1761 and 1762** in different passages. Stone corroborates volunteering and departure toward King's Mountain, not James's presence in the battle; Ellis reports shared service later in South Carolina. Parentage and any connection to Ashley or earlier James men are absent. [Analysis](../branches/blevins-revolutionary-james.md). |

Newell* abstract reproduced in [Shirley's study](https://yanceyfamilygenealogy.org/KenShirleyGenealogyBooks/blevins.pdf#page=168) cites new-series case **62**. | **Research lead only.** Shared number and Newell surname do not prove the 1812 file contains the Blevins petition. The index does not name Blevins; read the 99 scanned pages or obtain the actual Augusta County case before treating the abstract as original-record evidence. |
| B16 | [Original 1870 Ashe County census](../sources/records/shubal-adia-blevins-census-1870.jpg) · [FamilySearch viewer](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:S3HY-6S2Q-754?view=index&personArk=%2Fark%3A%2F61903%2F1%3A1%3AMW87-NM5&action=view&cc=1438024&lang=en), Piney Creek Township, p. 20, household 136: **Shubal Blevins, 22, farm work**, followed by **Adia Blevins, 30, keeping house** in John Thompson's household. John's **$450 real estate/$150 personal estate** values cannot be assigned to Shubal. Age implies about 1848 birth, conflicting with cemetery's 1844. |
| B17 | [Original 1900 Smyth County census](../sources/records/james-shubiel-ada-blevins-census-1900.jpg) · [FamilySearch viewer](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:S3HT-6737-4SW?view=index&personArk=%2Fark%3A%2F61903%2F1%3A1%3AMMJ4-3PY&action=view&lang=en), Marion District, sheet 43A/2, lines 10–16: head **James M. Blevins**; Shubiel **“Father,” 54, born April 1846**; Ada **“Mother,” 57**, 31 years married, **10 children born/6 living**. | Parent labels directly identify James M. as a son and document the older couple in Virginia by 1900. The 1846 birth year conflicts with the cemetery's 1844; the six surviving children are not all named here. The sheet does not explain the move or give a family fortune. |
| B18 | [Original 1850 Ashe County census](../sources/records/daniel-elizabeth-blevins-census-1850.jpg) · [FamilySearch viewer](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:S3HY-6QB3-SBM?view=index&personArk=%2Fark%3A%2F61903%2F1%3A1%3AM4BQ-XPM&action=view&lang=en), household 210. | Daniel, **50**, farmer, **$600 real estate**; Elizabeth, **40**; **Shubel, 6**, among younger household members. No relationship labels, so sonship is inferred. The child's age suggests c.1844, agreeing with the cemetery year but differing from 1860. An elderly **Nancy Bracins** appears; her relation is unstated. |
| B19 | [Original 1860 Alleghany County census, p. 85](../sources/records/daniel-elizabeth-blevins-census-1860-page85.jpg) · [p. 86 continuation](../sources/records/daniel-elizabeth-blevins-census-1860-page86.jpg) · [FamilySearch viewer p. 85](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:33SQ-GBS4-NN5?view=index&personArk=%2Fark%3A%2F61903%2F1%3A1%3AMDDL-G62&action=view&lang=en) · [p. 86](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:33SQ-GBS4-NR6?view=index&lang=en&groupId=M9CV-G3D), Gap Civil, 25 August. | Household **687/family 557** starts with Daniel, **58**, farmer, **$150 personal estate** and blank real-estate cell, and Elisabeth, **58**. **Shubel, 12**, is at the top of the next sheet, with **no new household number**. | B20 | [Original 1870 Alleghany County census](../sources/records/daniel-elizabeth-blevins-census-1870.jpg) · [FamilySearch viewer](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:S3HT-DY6S-KPP?view=index&personArk=%2Fark%3A%2F61903%2F1%3A1%3AMWZ5-7PQ&action=view&lang=en), Gap Civil, 16 September. | Daniel, **62**, farmer, **$200 real estate**, and Elizabeth, **50**, appear with Nancy, Polly, Sally, John and a younger Elizabeth. | V182 | [Original Milocca birth act no. 35](../sources/records/calogera-cordaro-birth-1900.jpg) · [FamilySearch image](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/3:1:3QSQ-G9MX-C4Q8?view=index&personArk=%2Fark%3A%2F61903%2F1%3A1%3AXQ75-4C2V&action=view&lang=en) · [readable crop](../media/calogera-cordaro-birth-1900-detail.jpg). | The act was drawn **22 June 1900**. **Antonio Cordaro**, age 23, *contadino* (farm worker), reported daughter **Calogera**, female, born **19 June** at about 8:30 a.m., to wife **Pietra Magro** in Milocca. The [first FamilySearch index](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:XQ75-4C2V?lang=en) says **29 June**, apparently a transcription error; a [second index](https://www.familysearch.org/ark:/61903/1:1:QV98-GRHP?lang=en) agrees with the handwritten **19 June**. Antonio's later petition and Fannie's card give **18 June**, so retain the conflict. Original scan saved unaltered; crop for reading. |

House marked **owned** and valued by the household at **$4,600**; Steve was a **laborer in a coal mine**. This is a reported property value, not a deed or net worth. The surname is spelled *Matina* here; spouse, ages and children match their other records. |

The birth day conflicts with V182's original act. Card is an official index, not the full petition/certificate. |

It corrects the earlier uncertainty about this specific household's 1930 address/value; another sheet 2B household's **$7,000** belongs elsewhere. Downloaded unaltered through signed-in FamilySearch, 28 September 2026. |

Mulhall**, shows a labeled contemporary portrait, and says he was Ireland-born, a house painter, naturalized in Washington and appointed to the DC police **15 Aug 1871**. J. E. Mulhall** to 3 March 1913 assembly points and directs 4 sergeants, 40 privates and 40 special policemen to report to him at Garfield Monument, **1:30 p.m.** This documents an assignment, not his personal actions or views. NPS supplies context for the procession and force-wide crowd-control failure; it does not evaluate Mulhall individually. |

Smith** attended about **30–35 years earlier**, yielding an approximate **1980–89** search window. S1 names Timothy A. as Alice Smith Weatherford's brother, supporting the stated uncle relationship. | Family account for attendance and Ashley's years; Timothy's date range is an approximation from a relative interval. No program, campus record, degree or commencement evidence supplied. Do not match either to a same-named student without another identifier. |

Search the E1 windows and inspect scanned pages; a yearbook appearance alone does not prove graduation. |

It offers a way to test John/Edison and Alice/Hayfield family accounts; it does not verify their individual attendance. |
| E4 | [Coughlin High School alumni index, K](https://coughlinhighschool.org/alumni-list-k.html) and [separate class-finder listing](https://www.classfinders.com/directory/pa/wilkes-barre/15/) both list **Paul Kramer, class of 1982**. | Third-party/name-only alumni listings, possibly sharing a data source. This agrees with the shared chat's school/approximate years, but neither page identifies the student as Justin's father or supplies a yearbook image. **Lead, not a certified graduation.** |
| E5 | G.A.R. Memorial High School, *Garchive*, 1940, [senior page 21](https://www.e-yearbook.com/yearbooks/G_A_R_Memorial_High_School_Garchive_Yearbook/1940/Page_21.html); [public OCR of the volume](https://www.e-yearbook.com/yearbooks/G_A_R_Memorial_High_School_Garchive_Yearbook/1940/Page_1.html). | The site's school-scoped search for “Hilda Kramer” returns **1940, page 21**; the OCR names **Hilda Kramer, Commercial** in the senior portraits. Checked in Chrome 27 September 2026. A contemporary school source strongly identifying the person when paired with U2 and U17, but the image viewer requires membership and was not inspected. The site expressly bars copying its material without authorization, so no scan was saved. Obituary U2, rather than the visible OCR, reports graduation and subsequent insurance work. |

**Source discipline:** Do not turn the shared chat's “strong” match labels into proof. Do not attach a source to an unrelated person with the same name. Do not turn historic mill ownership, modern photos, or local events into claims about an ancestor's personal experience.
